# ECHO MCP interactions journal — June 2026 shard

This is the **June 2026 monthly shard** of ECHO's cross-tool MCP-call journal. Entries land here from 2026-06-01 onward. The prior shard is frozen at `mcp-interactions-journal-2026-05.md`; do not append to it. The historical monolith is frozen at `mcp-interactions-journal-archive-through-2026-05-17.md`.

**Originating item:** [`2026-05-06-018-recent-work-context-tool`](../../../backlog/complete/2026-05-06-018-recent-work-context-tool.md)
**Sources active in store:** claude-code, codex, cursor, git
**Timezone convention:** all times in this journal are **founder's local time (PDT, America/Los_Angeles)** unless explicitly noted. Source data stores ISO 8601 UTC; entries here are converted on write.

## Quick-Fill Template

```
### YYYY-MM-DD HH:MM PDT — <one-line context>

- **Trigger:** <why the tool was called>
- **Query inputs:** <tool(args), one line or compact numbered list>
- **Returned:** <N clusters/M atoms, N matches, N turns, warnings, top label/rank reasons>
- **Sources:** <source_breakdown | source_resolved | per-match prefixes | exact paths>
- **Verdict:** <✅ right | 🟡 partial | ❌ wrong> — <short reason>
- **Note:** <what felt useful/off>
- **Conjecture:** <optional>
```

## Rotation Rule

On the first MCP-call journal append of each new calendar month, create `mcp-interactions-journal-YYYY-MM.md` with this preamble template and update `CLAUDE.md`'s current-shard pointer in the same commit.

### 2026-06-03 15:39 PDT — strategist monitors the codex builder (087b) for friction/failure-retry
- **Trigger:** founder asked Claude (strategist/watcher) to monitor the live Codex builder run on 087b, paying extra attention to friction points and failure/retry.

- **Returned:** call #1 → **transport ERROR ("operation timed out")** before the server's 60s hold elapsed — the client MCP transport limit is < 60s. call #2 (timeout=25) → normal empty shape `{turn_ids:[], timed_out:true, next_since:"2026-06-03T22:39:30.637Z"}`.
- **Sources:** `mcp__echo.wait_for_new_turns` against local daemon; codex source, no atoms returned (no builder turns captured since claim 22:34:50Z → 22:39:30Z). Builder worktree exists + clean tree (verified via git, not MCP): builder is in read-spec_refs phase, no code/commits yet.
- **Verdict:** 🟡 partial — the wait primitive works at ≤25s but the builder's long read/plan turn hasn't flushed to capture, so live-turn monitoring is blind until the first codex turn completes; git-polling the worktree is the better progress signal meanwhile.
- **Note:** **Friction:** `wait_for_new_turns(timeout=60)` exceeds the MCP client transport timeout and surfaces as an error rather than the documented empty-return — a monitoring footgun. Keep timeout ≤ ~25–30s. (The tool's own max is 60, but the client can't hold that long here.)
- **Conjecture:** (obs only) either clamp the advertised max to the client transport budget, or have the wrapper chunk a long wait into ≤25s server holds — so callers don't have to know the transport limit.

(2) 1 cluster of 12 (11 dropped by limit): rank-1 `ctx_b871bae9` "work on Project_echo", 500 atoms (trunc 200), source_breakdown={git:384, claude_code:222} — Project_echo-scoped, MISSED the windows_onboard thread. (3) 3 matches: Jun5 backlog-rediscovery + Jun4 07:39 dev-vs-release CI decision (windows_onboard) + Jun4 07:22 Haiku subagent scoping echoctl conventions. (4) 2 matches: Jun5 rediscovery + Jun4 decision turn naming branch agent/onboarding-ci. - **Sources:** find_clusters → git:/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo (384) + claude_code Project_echo sessions (222); **codex+cursor+windows_onboard silently absent from the rank-1 cluster** (repo-scoped cluster missed the cross-project thread). search_memories → `fs:…/.claude/projects/-Users-zhenye-Desktop-Project-echo/d5d732c0…` + `fs:…/-Users-zhenye-Desktop-windows-onboard/{66cdbbd5…,d65d86ed…}.jsonl` + `subagents/{agent-a1e80a16…,a575d…,ae030…,a659c…,a6d0f…,aa238d…,af04ac…}.jsonl`. Cross-project + subagent capture is what cracked the thread; no codex/git atoms in the windows slice.
- **Verdict:** 🟡 partial overall — `find_clusters` (❌-leaning 🟡: one repo-scoped mega-cluster swallowed the window and missed the actual cross-project thread); `search_memories` (✅): literal-token "selftest"/"onboarding-ci" + a windows_onboard `source_prefix` scope cleanly isolated the authoring/decision/rediscovery turns across two project dirs + 7 subagents.
- **Note:** The win was cross-project + subagent capture surfacing a thread that lives OUTSIDE the Project_echo repo (in `~/Desktop/windows_onboard` Claude Code sessions) — exactly the cross-tool/cross-session value prop. `find_clusters` alone would have missed it because its top cluster is repo-scoped; `search_memories` with explicit source_prefix is what reached the foreign project dir. Two recurring footguns reconfirmed: (a) TZ-less `since`/`until` warns and is ambiguous; (b) oversized responses (>~85KB) spill to a tool-results file requiring a parse fallback.
- **Conjecture:** (observations only) cluster ranking that's strictly repo-scoped under-serves cross-project recall; a "selftest"-style literal token + foreign source_prefix was needed to reach the other project — worth noting whether find_clusters should ever surface a secondary cross-project cluster instead of N near-duplicates of the same repo cluster.

### 2026-06-05 14:20 PDT — coord_status read while diagnosing whether 092 r1 reviewers would fire

- **Trigger:** after dispatching 092 r1 the active-trigger `coord_invoke` failed (ENOENT, already journaled); called `coord_status` to judge whether the launchd-fallback reviewers would still pick up the round.
- **Query inputs:** `coord_status()` (no args).
- **Returned:** open_deadlines=1 (codex `scheduler_health`); per_role_last_tick — codex `last_tick` 20:55:01→20:55:02Z (**1 sec**), codex-ops 20:33→20:34Z (41s); recent_missed=11 (all codex `scheduler_health`); daemon_uptime≈184267s.
- **Sources:** coord substrate atoms (daemon-side); `per_role_last_tick` + `open_deadlines` aggregation.
- **Verdict:** 🟡 partial — data was accurate, but the **1-second codex `last_tick` (which fired AFTER my r1 push) misled me into suspecting the launchd reviewer was no-opping/broken.** Manually running the wrapper disproved that — `codex exec` reviewed r1 fine and pushed `codex.md` ~5 min later. The 1-sec tick was a `scheduler_health` heartbeat / pre-propagation no-op, not a review tick.
- **Note:** `coord_status` `per_role_last_tick` conflates `scheduler_health` heartbeats with actual review ticks under one `last_tick_start/end` pair; a reader can't distinguish "reviewer ran and found nothing" from "scheduler heartbeat" from "reviewer errored fast." For the question "will my just-dispatched round get reviewed?", coord_status was insufficient — the ground-truth signal was the response file landing on `origin/main`. (Whole 092 run, r1→r3, converged via the manual-wrapper fallback; active-trigger never worked this session.)
- **Conjecture:** (optional) a per-role `last_review_tick` distinct from `last_scheduler_health`, or an `event_type` tag on `last_tick`, would let coord_status answer "is the reviewer healthy for THIS request" without a manual wrapper run.

### 2026-06-06 12:02 PDT — action-grounded retrieval weakness probes
- **Trigger:** Founder challenged a code-based retrieval critique and asked for action-grounded probes that expose weak cases, with root causes logged.
- **Query inputs:** `search_memories(source_app=claude_code, repo_path=/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo, query="detail walk through", limit=10)`; `search_memories(... query="walkthrough", limit=5)`; `search_memories(... query="normalize trace rank", limit=10)`; `search_memories(... query="normalize→trace→rank", limit=10)`; `find_clusters(repo_path=/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo, since=2026-06-06T18:00:00.000Z, until=2026-06-06T19:00:00.000Z, window_hours=4, format=skeleton, view=rich)`; `get_atoms(11 ids from both clusters, prefer=newest_first, fields=["content"], view=compact)`; `get_recent_work_context(same window, format=minimal, limit=20)`.
- **Returned — exact-query brittleness:** Verbatim miss shape: `"query":"detail walk through" → "matches":[],"total_returned":0`. Nearest successful query: `"query":"walkthrough" → id=6159cf3f-6ed0-41bd-8d44-046471a58ee4` at `2026-06-06T18:30:54.636Z`. Second miss shape: `"query":"normalize trace rank" → "matches":[],"total_returned":0`; exact Unicode form `"normalize→trace→rank" → id=6159cf3f-6ed0-41bd-8d44-046471a58ee4`.
- **Root cause — exact-query brittleness:** `search_memories.query` is literal case-insensitive substring matching. It does not tokenize, normalize separators, stem, or semantic-match. Human paraphrases and ASCII fallback text miss even when the intended memory is present.
- **Returned — ranking/source-balance tradeoff:** `find_clusters` over 18:00-19:00Z returned cluster rank 1 `ctx_b9d6cb4d`, label `"discussion about Project_echo"`, `source_breakdown={"claude_code":6}`, `time_range=18:12:30→18:30:54`, `rank_reason=["recent_activity","has_open_loop","has_unresolved_open_loop","code_session_anchor","dense"]`. Rank 2 was `ctx_2b985fd1`, label `"discussion about ECHO"`, `source_breakdown={"codex":5}`, `time_range=18:39:47→18:55:50`, `rank_reason=["recent_activity","code_session_anchor","dense"]`. `get_atoms(prefer=newest_first)` confirmed the rank-2 cluster contained the newer current Codex thread, including the founder question `"can you evaluate my retrieval strategy and logic"` at `18:55:50Z`.
- **Root cause — ranking/source-balance tradeoff:** The ranker correctly followed its heuristic, but the result is user-intent ambiguous: unresolved/open-loop + density made the older Claude walkthrough outrank the newer Codex thread. This is acceptable for "what open loop is live?" but weak for "what is the latest/current activity?" unless the caller narrows the source or explicitly uses `get_atoms(prefer=newest_first)` over candidate clusters.
- **Returned — join-key weakness:** `get_recent_work_context` exposed anchor artifacts. Claude cluster rank 1 anchors included `{"type":"repo","provider":"local","id":"local:/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo"}` and conversation `claude_code:5fd2bd27-...`. Codex cluster rank 2 anchors included `{"type":"repo","provider":"github","id":"https://github.com/zhenye0616/ECHO","locator":"/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo"}` and conversation `codex:019e9e39-...`. Same local checkout, same repo_path filter, but different normalized repo artifact IDs.
- **Root cause — join-key weakness:** `repo_path` scoped candidates but did not become the common join artifact. Claude normalized the repo as `local:/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo` because the captured Claude `git_state` lacked `origin_url`; Codex normalized it as GitHub remote because Codex metadata had `git.origin_url`. The shared-artifact graph therefore split related Claude and Codex turns into two clusters. This is stronger evidence than the earlier code-only critique: cross-tool handoff can fragment when repo identity differs by adapter/source metadata.
- **Returned — diagnostic-surface weakness:** In the compound minimal response, Codex cluster `ctx_2b985fd1` had `edges: []` even though its atoms formed one cluster. The reason is not missing clustering; displayed edges filter out scope/session-only edges, while cluster membership still used conversation/repo artifacts internally.
- **Root cause — diagnostic-surface weakness:** The user-visible diagnostic surface can hide the actual reason a cluster exists. For debugging retrieval, ECHO needs a compact "joined_by" / "membership_artifacts" explanation separate from filtered UI edges.
- **Verdict:** right and actionable. The action probes confirmed three real weaknesses: literal-query brittleness, rank ambiguity between open-loop and freshness, and cross-tool cluster fragmentation caused by inconsistent repo artifact identity.
- **Conjecture:** Highest-leverage fixes before semantic search: canonicalize repo artifacts across adapters using `repo_root` + late git remote lookup; expose cluster `joined_by` keys; add a caller-facing mode or rank option for `latest_activity` vs `open_loop_resume`; add lightweight query normalization for separators (`walk through`/`walkthrough`, `->`/`→`).

### 2026-06-06 12:05 PDT — additional retrieval weakness probe matrix + report
- **Trigger:** Founder asked to run a couple more rounds with different variations and gather all results in a report.
- **Query inputs:** `search_memories(source_app=claude_code, repo_path=/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo, query="current progress", limit=10)`; `search_memories(... query="current progress", since=2026-06-06T18:00:00.000Z, until=2026-06-06T19:00:00.000Z)`; `search_memories(... query="current ptogrss", same window)`; `find_clusters(repo_path=/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo, format=skeleton, view=rich)`; `get_recent_work_context(since=2026-06-06T18:00:00.000Z, until=2026-06-06T19:10:00.000Z, artifact_hint={provider:local,type:repo,id:local:/Users/zhenye/Desktop/Project_echo}, format=skeleton)`; `get_recent_work_context(same window, artifact_hint={provider:github,type:repo,id:https://github.com/zhenye0616/ECHO}, format=skeleton)`; `search_memories(... query="cognitive debt months sprinting", same 18:00-19:00 window)`; `search_memories(... query="cognitive debt from months of sprinting", same window)`; `get_atoms(12 ids from Claude+Codex clusters, prefer=newest_first, format=minimal, view=rich)`.
- **Returned — typo/fuzzy recall:** Broad `query="current progress"` returned older unrelated matches, newest `03c7c98a-a1e9-49ce-a896-bc7f80c54a19` at `2026-06-03T22:41:35.991Z`. Narrowed to the walkthrough hour, `query="current progress"` returned 0. Captured typo `query="current ptogrss"` returned `5644a051-37e4-4e46-a0ef-ac0d44b671e2` with verbatim user text `current ptogrss`.
- **Root cause — typo/fuzzy recall:** No typo tolerance; broad literal search can return plausible but wrong older hits unless time/source constraints are tight.
- **Returned — default ranking variation:** No-args repo-scoped `find_clusters` used `since=2026-06-06T15:05:50.900Z` and `until=2026-06-06T19:05:50.900Z`. It still ranked Claude cluster `ctx_b9d6cb4d` first (`claude_code:6`, open-loop reasons) and newer Codex cluster `ctx_6fc1b539` second (`codex:6`, no open-loop reasons).
- **Root cause — default ranking variation:** Default resume behavior is open-loop-biased; it is not a "latest activity" mode.
- **Returned — artifact-hint split:** Local repo artifact hint returned only Claude (`source_breakdown={"claude_code":6}`); GitHub repo artifact hint returned only Codex (`source_breakdown={"codex":6}`), even with the same `repo_path` and time window.
- **Root cause — artifact-hint split:** Same as prior finding, now isolated by artifact_hint: local path repo identity and GitHub remote repo identity are different graph keys.
- **Returned — concept compression:** `query="cognitive debt months sprinting"` returned 0; exact captured phrase `query="cognitive debt from months of sprinting"` returned `c3de87b9-b0a9-4554-aa56-c5740c0d1e7f`.
- **Root cause — concept compression:** No token-based AND fallback; removing stopwords breaks contiguous substring matching.
- **Returned — body hydration budget:** `get_atoms` over 12 IDs without `fields` projection returned 7 atoms and dropped 5 (`3dc3dd13...`, `0b8d30f0...`, `c3de87b9...`, `e921c2da...`, `5644a051...`) under the 25k envelope. Large returned atoms carried `truncations=["content","metadata.tool_calls:projected"]`.
- **Root cause — body hydration budget:** Expected prefix-drop behavior, but operationally important: multi-cluster body hydration must be chunked or projected (`fields=["content"]`) before full-body recovery.
- **Report:** Wrote the combined report to `raw/internal/dogfooding/2026-06-06-retrieval-weakness-probe-report.md`.
- **Verdict:** right — additional rounds reinforced the earlier findings and added two concrete variants: typo/noisy-text failure and hydration-budget drop behavior.

Also surfaced a live daemon-health issue (`Ajv` import/runtime mismatch) separate from retrieval quality.

### 2026-06-06 20:52 PDT — correction: daemon is healthy; Ajv was stale-log noise
- **Trigger:** Founder asked to dive into the root cause of the prior caveat: raw HTTP ECHO unhealthy / `Ajv is not a constructor`.
- **Query inputs:** Local probes: `loadCoordRoles()` under repo `vite-node`; installed package `dist/coord/roles.js` under the plist Node binary; `echoctl daemon status`; `launchctl print`; raw MCP `initialize` curls with protocol `2024-11-05` and `2025-06-18`; host-level `ps/lsof/netstat` after sandbox process/socket checks were denied.
- **Returned:** Current repo `vite-node` and installed package both load Ajv/coord roles successfully (`codex,codex-ops,claude,cursor`). Launchd reports PID 28052. Host-level diagnostics show PID 28052 is `/usr/local/Cellar/node@22/22.22.1_1/bin/node .../echoctl/dist/daemon/index.js` and owns `127.0.0.1:38478 (LISTEN)`. Host-level curl to `/mcp` returns HTTP 200 and protocol `2025-06-18`; host-level `echoctl daemon status --json` returns `health:"healthy"`. The `Ajv is not a constructor` stack lives in `~/Library/Logs/echo/daemon.err.log` from an older dev/vite-node path, while the active launchd stderr `echo-daemon.err.log` is empty and active stdout has a fresh `mcp.server started` line at `2026-06-07T03:43:28.854Z`.
- **Sources:** Raw HTTP MCP endpoint `http://127.0.0.1:38478/mcp`; process/socket table for PID 28052; active launchd logs `~/Library/Logs/echo/echo-daemon.out.log`/`.err.log`; stale historical dev log `~/Library/Logs/echo/daemon.err.log`; source files `src/coord/roles.ts`, `src/cli/commands/daemon.ts`, `src/mcp/server.ts`.
- **Verdict:** ✅ right correction — the live daemon is healthy from the host. The earlier caveat was wrong: it conflated stale historical Ajv logs with sandboxed localhost/proc diagnostics failing from Codex's restricted command environment.
- **Note:** Root cause is diagnostic-environment mismatch + stale-log ambiguity, not an ECHO runtime import bug. Future daemon-health investigations should prefer `echo-daemon.*` for launchd, not `daemon.*`, and should distinguish sandboxed command failures from host-level health before declaring the daemon broken.
